=== As We Leave #74 :  Looking Back, For Now ===
Source: https://voxiitk.com/as-we-leave-74-looking-back-for-now/
Date: 2026-07-18

In this edition of As We Leave,  Ritik Shah, a Y22 student in the Department of Electrical Engineering  looks back at his four years at IITK. He reflects on a journey that evolved from initial hesitation and strict boundaries to finding true connection in crowded dorm rooms and late-night walks. It’s a story about navigating the chaotic highs and lows of campus life, weathering missed opportunities, and discovering that the friendships forged here form an unbreakable thread.

I think I do not intend the AWL to carry any message, but rather that it be a record of the 4 years and the emotions I have felt at IIT Kanpur and at my hostel rooms – which by the 2nd year I had begun calling “ghar”. Decades down the line when I look back at this same experience – I will undoubtedly feel differently about it.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #74 : Looking Back, For Now]
The 1 month or so prior to 27 October  2022 was pure bliss – everything was done and enjoyment was the only thing in mind. Joining IITK was tumultuous – it began a sequence of events and actions which through highs and lows would allow me to understand myself more, improve myself (and gain some bad habits along the way).

Hum wingies ke beechme pehle hesitation tha obviously, which was made a little worse for me because of my extra strict “Don’t’s” list arising from something like germaphobia. Yet eventually, these were the same wingies jinke saath movies dekhte raat me and jaha 6 launde 2 bed pe sote (we probably agreed to not tell others about this, but well !). Also…like a tryhard…. I signed up for both Techkriti and E-Cell right in the 1st year/1st semester and soon that led to reduced time with wingies – but also discovery of other fun and tryhard people across campus. In the midst of all the chaos of 1st year, I also found some reliable and calming friends across the road in H4. End of 1st year was also when late night walks began with Rashi and other great friends- these walks, I will very dearly miss and try to replicate wherever I go – they give a true sense of freedom found nowhere else.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #74 : Looking Back, For Now]
With barely any time between the semesters and after the 1st year – IIT Kanpur was just as much comfortable as home and 2nd year was set to be much easier, well that was until I planned to take 3-4 PoRs simultaneously, although EE ke 3rd sem acads are fairly chill. It was through ICG that I met more like minded people, and interacted with some folks in administration. I mostly treated my 2nd year as a time of finding the right club/team for me – and guess what, I did not make it to the 3rd year PoR at the only one I applied to  (ICG). Obviously it was one of the worse times at college – with most of my friends making it to their respective clubs. What kept me sane through all this was the friends and putting efforts towards one thing I could do well – in this case acads. Also just because everyone was doing it  – I took up SURGE – hoping to explore 5G Communications. This began a year-long association with a faculty – one I never fully utilized and never gave it the efforts it deserved and yet pulled through because of some great folks in the Lab like Yogesh. Looking back – I realised the importance of exploring because I discovered what I didn’t like – even though it took me a year to understand that Communications was not for me.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #74 : Looking Back, For Now]
One of the most ironic situations was during the intern season – poora summer I had done barely any coding, DSA, CP etc (even to this day I barely know the difference between how one preps for DSA and for CP) – stressed out over it lot during OAs- only to not hear back from any of these companies and end up at HUL. The kind of selfless guidance that some seniors like Raghav gave then and how much he has guided till today can’t be stressed upon enough. Raghav was the first senior at IITK who made me realise what giving back to juniors truly means.  In the quest to find something outside academics and spending time with friends – I ended up on the DUGC – a relatively lonely PoR which was made fun only by Amay and the shenanigans that some students/faculty pulled. I remain glad for this role not just because of the work (which was often not very great – wdym that 10 people ask the same goddamn doubt even though you sent a mail regarding it??) but also because I am leaving with a much better understanding of the system and the folks that govern us students. I particularly remember the gyan chodi sessions we gave to others or the gyan chodi profs gave to us. But quite a few faculty were great, understanding and open to listening to us and talking about random stuff.  A new roommate is always a new adventure and so was my time with Mudit. The late night gaming sessions and we used to invite people from other halls. EE330 ki exam ghatiya gayi?   F**k it we ball karke play road redemption till I couldn’t keep my eyes open – im glad Mudit and Vighnesh pushed me to resume playing games – its a core memory ill carry.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #74 : Looking Back, For Now]
Fast forward to last year – can’t really believe it’s done. As I sit at home and write this, the feeling of leaving college is not fully set in. People often say it hits the most during convocation and so I remain glad for the 1.5-2 months in between where I still feel like I will return to college anytime and go to Zing with the group ya fir Hall 7 ke canteen mein random movie pe jokes karenge. I found some great people with similar interests through courses in the last 2 semesters – something which I hadn’t expected.  7th sem was chaos for those in placements but this is only  part 1 – when almost half of your group plans to go for masters or Gate you realise ki chaos doesnt end there lol. The Mussoorie trip in midst of all this – remains on top.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #74 : Looking Back, For Now]
Kaafi kaafi saari cheeze have been missed over here – primarily because I am a little hesitant of putting very personal stuff here. Some great people I found across the college, brief brushes with politics, great supportive faculty and  many friends who played a vital role in my stay – Arush, Shreya, Shruti, Vighnesh other than those named above. Almost every testimonial I wrote ended with “i hope our paths cross again” – because I truly hope for that and a part of me strongly believes that our paths will cross (i hope this isnt delulu but probably is). IIT Kanpur is a common thread connecting us – and the 4 years here make one feel that the thread is unbreakable.

Signing off – RVS

Written by: Ritk Shah

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #74 : Looking Back, For Now]
Edited by: Dishita Bajaj, Krishna Khetre

=== As We Leave #75 : How to be a Mahaarajan ===
Source: https://voxiitk.com/as-we-leave-75-how-to-be-a-mahaarajan/
Date: 2026-07-24

In this edition of As We Leave, Mahaarajan J a Y22 student from the Department of Computer Science and Engineering, takes us through his four years at IIT Kanpur, kind of like a timeline with detours. He recalls the friendships, clubs, those academic milestones, and the travels that really shaped his college life. Starting with finding a home in DebSoc and Vox, and then wandering around different destinations across India, he talks about a journey that was powered by spontaneity and a lot of happiness, also by simply making the most of every opportunity that appeared.

Hi, well I am Mahaarajan which would be pretty obvious from the title. So this AWL here is gonna be a bit fun and a really frank insight into my 4 years of college and maybe u can draw parallels or take inspiration from it. If anything I would like you to be happier and continue on with a smile on your face. Like all AWLs this shall be a memoir of my college journey, an ode to this absolutely magical 4 (3.5 years) of my life. So, sit back, relax, and come on board on this journey with me. This is my AWL piece  and I have put down my thoughts as it is, apologies if this does not read well 🙂

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #75 : How to be a Mahaarajan]
So when I arrived on campus I did not expect much, It was a Chennai boy in “North India” for crying out loud. I did not know anyone but that changed quickly when I met my first and still one of my best friends Dinesh, and suddenly things did not feel as daunting 🙂 We then had our wing bakchodi, harlem shake was OP man. We were happy that time. Also did anchoring in Freshers night, wearing a suit and being on that oat in front of the whole batch was surreal. I got into squash and met Viral with whom I have had some legendary matches.

Just like that in the wink of an eye with ded saara padhai and parties 1st year got over and I got a feel of what college life is like. I learned to be happy all the time (I still am) and do a lot of crazy stuff like super long cycle rides on 0 sleep, skywalk cycling at night and so on. This became my life philosophy, work hard and enjoy harder.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #75 : How to be a Mahaarajan]
Being an SG was a peak experience, getting to experience this orientation from behind the scenes was so so fun. And the bakchodi with other SGs was also lovely. If you are reading this and thinking of whether to fill that form or not, fill krle mere bhai kaafi maza aata hai. I was a DebSoc secy, this has been one club that has stuck with me till the end, has the best and craziest people, made good memories with many of my best friends there like Zehaan, The V and Pranav 🙂 , It was more of a family than a club.

I had been an  AssEd at Vox too where I worked on many interesting articles with my mates like the Language barriers one with Sruthi or the construction workers one with Pranav or the PhD article with Utkarsh. Vox gave me an opportunity to explore my passion for writing and is partly the reason I am writing this AWL today, I am thankful to Vox for giving me the cutest seniors in the form of Utkarsh, Gauravi, Mutasim, Vrinda and Mohika. ELS was also fun and during my time we were almost the same group of people everywhere 🙂 so even club work was more of a fun hangout.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #75 : How to be a Mahaarajan]
Second year was when the idea of “Mahaarajan” started to concretise. I used to handle my academics almost effortlessly while being involved in multiple places and “Mahaarajan” became synonymous with me being down for anything always with a happy attitude .With more of my CSE courses coming in I was getting better and acads became more fun than a burden. Huge thanks to the department for all those memorable courses. And this was when I found my gang through one huge ass assignment (Manindra bro)  and Elysium, my closest friends for the next 2 years Akanksha, Animesh, Sankalp, Kushagra, Aditi, Arush, Ritesh, Amul and Jagdale.

This was also the time I started going on college trips starting with IITB, oh man oh man the hike to the hill and the forest fire. The lore is too heavy to pen down here but do ask ur DebSoc seniors about it, Followed by a beautiful and calming trip to God’s own country, exploring Kochi. Will remember this trip for that 5h long boat ride in Kumarakom with the beautiful sunset and thunder while returning, kites by the beach and driving a rental for the first time :).  Special mentions to the national anthem in Bombay and the speed bump on the ‘highway’ near Kochi. I have become a much better driver now ig 🙂

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #75 : How to be a Mahaarajan]
Then we had the Mimamsa trip to Pune, I was truly amazed with how much bio I knew (We topped in Bio in the prelims). I shall remember Anantha and playing ball inside the room with Sruthi, Viral and Dinesh. And the way we handled the questions by going up to the stage and confidently explaining something which we were 0% sure of was crazy. Walking around IISER with Viral at night shall remain a core memory. Special mentions to the dog that saved us brdr and Anantha our literal guide and of course Krishna from Logistics :).

Rafting was a vibe with Ritesh (my first Bro-moon) after the sem got over, man those crazy scooty rides on the slopes in Mussoire and highways of Haridwar. This was truly living life to the fullest. Special mentions to the night drive after dinner on the highway and losing my glasses in the Ganges. Remember guys always say yes to trips, it has been one of the best parts of my college life (ironically outside college lol).

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #75 : How to be a Mahaarajan]
Onto sem 5, after a grueling summer prepping for interns and finally getting through Day-0 with Quadeye was really satisfying. After this I thought I had experienced everything there is and my life couldn’t get much better. This is what you need to understand, you never peak, things only get better as you move ahead. In solving those difficult Lab questions set by Deba I found someone far greater any experience I ever had, it sure did take me a hell lot of courage :). This was also the first sem where I got a true taste of core CSE thru OS, TOC et all and man that was fun. At the end of this I got 10 people onboard for a crazzzy Goa trip by booking non-refundable flights (note to the planners out there) before planning anything. We were a lovely group and the trip was absolutely magical, one of the best trips I have executed. Special mention to the paragliding and butterfly beach :). That lovely sunset at Gamyam just like the sunset at La Manzanilla in Rosarita 🙂

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #75 : How to be a Mahaarajan]
The internship in the upcoming summer was great, the intern taught me to be more confident in myself and the people around me. Always be open to learn and never be afraid of being uncomfortable to learn, appreciate stepping out of your comfort zone once in a while. Interesting fact, I met Tarun whose AWL I edited in 2nd year over at Quadeye :). Was absolutely elated with the return offer and off I went into my final year starting with a Dad trip to Zambia followed by the MVM gang reuniting in the beaches of Bombay. So my last year was filled with good courses, lovely trips…Now I shall delve into my trips one by one, I shall try to keep it brief but be ready 🙂

I ended up visiting Ladakh with Shivam and Bhutan with Sankalp in the 3rd year. Both the places were insanely beautiful and showed me how majestic nature can get. Travelling is life ma man, to travel is to be free. Solo trips might not be that fun but don’t hesitate to go even if u have only one more person with you.  The stars in Ladakh and the peace in the monasteries of Bhutan made me appreciate life more, I was genuinely happy to have experienced these places in this life. Both the trips were really gutsy and most of it was not pre-planned, it is those unplanned adventures that are most memorable :). Special mentions to Malabari Banger & getting scolded by the British couple in Ladakh. Also the Baileys, Holi in ice and the crazy winds that day in Bhutan 🙂

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #75 : How to be a Mahaarajan]
The final year was a happy continuation of how life was at sem 6, I felt whole and was well and truly happy with everything. I also went on 2 short trips to Agra and Jaipur in the meanwhile on weekends to make the most of the time I had left. Courses this time was completely my choice and boy I had a blast, challenged myself academically with many tough courses and emerged victorious on the other side. The fests were also great as everyone came together for one last dance.

The tripping only became better as I explored Meghalaya, Varkala and Munnar this time. The north east was truly mesmerising, the waterfalls there were so majestic and water so so clean and blue. It was one of the closest I have been to nature. I shall always remember that blue lagoon trek and everyone floating in Krem Chympe. Special mention to the boating in Shillong park on the last day, climbing after dark from blue lagoon. My return to God’s own country was nothing less as I enjoyed my birthday in the waters of the Arabian sea. The scooty rides we had with the open ocean to my left in Varkala and through the tea plantation in the morning in Munnar are stuff of dreams. Never should you stop travelling, what are we but travellers through this life.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #75 : How to be a Mahaarajan]
I also finally ended up going on a trek with my man Sankalp and it was one of the most challenging and transformative experiences of my life. Would recommend everyone to give it a try, the experience is truly something else. We walked for about 65km over 6 days in the steep slopes in the Indo-Nepal border. I felt one with nature and completely connected to my surroundings. In this digital age it is required that we disconnect every once a while and experience the nature in this world. I would hopefully continue this and make it tradition to trek once every year :).

Then it all culminated in the batch video preparations, photoshoots and farewells. Yes change is the only constant and I shall miss this place that I called home for the past 4 years, but I do look to the future with the same hopeful eyes that I had when I came to IITK with all those dreams and expectations. The one thing I would miss are the wonderful people and the freedom here on campus, I can walk in the middle of the road at 3am in the night which would be impossible in Gurgaon or elsewhere. I shall be forever grateful to IITK for all the experiences and friends :). I’m better off in all possible ways and am truly happy to have what I have after this absolutely wonderful 4 yr journey of mine. Yes there were moments when I struggled, But in the end it is only the happy moments that you will remember :). So create many many happy moments so that when you look back, you can say with full conviction and confidence that yes I have lived my college life.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #75 : How to be a Mahaarajan]
If you have been able to read it this far, thanks buddy for sitting through it, so just be happy stuff works out in the end, trust yourself you can make it, take it step by step and learn to take a break and travel the world.

“Mahaarajan” shall always persist and stand for this spontaneity, carefree happiness and optimism forever. Sometimes even I motivate myself to be more “Mahaarajan”. Have a lovely day ahead reader and experience the little things that make life worth living. All the best 🫶.

=== As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time ===
Source: https://voxiitk.com/as-we-leave-76-until-supper-time/
Date: 2026-07-28

In the 76th edition of As We Leave, Harshita Chhuttani takes us through her four years at IIT Kanpur, kind of like a timeline with detours. She recalls the deep friendships, campus clubs, academic highs and lows, and spontaneous moments that truly shaped her college life. Starting with navigating her first year on her cycle and finding a sense of purpose and home in ICS, to balancing placement stress with ukulele lessons and trips with friends, she talks about a journey of self-discovery, resilience, and learning to navigate life’s playground with empathy and joy.

There is no easy way to write this.
Here I am, 4 years later, sitting in the same chair where I spent almost two years preparing for the very 4 years I am now trying to write about.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
The room is familiar. The chair is familiar. Even the view outside the window hasn’t changed much.
Today, I find myself back in it, staring at a blank page, trying to make sense of everything that happened in between.
IIT was a place I knew only through newspaper headlines, success stories, and other people’s expectations. I had no idea why I wanted it or studied for it.

I came to college with a few suitcases, a rank and an email from the institute. I could never have imagined what this place would do to me.
As I left behind the JEE phase of my life, where my world was mostly online, I had very little experience with the kind of people interactions that college would throw me into.
To most people, I probably came across as rather reserved or introverted – someone who enjoyed her own company and preferred less social-battery-draining activities. Friendships were never something I sought out. I had friends, enough of them, but I believed I would be fine with or without them.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
I’d credit IIT Kanpur for slowly dismantling the assumptions I had.

This place gives you an identity that’s far beyond yourself. You become connected to people without even realizing it, you’re intertwined before you know it and they begin to matter more than you ever imagined. It’s almost unfair that I don’t have the sole control over my life the way I thought I did but it’s beautiful how I get to share it with people who make it so much more colourful.

IITK has given me a home where I’m safe enough to walk anywhere, anytime without a second thought. I’ve travelled every part of the campus so many times I feel like the roads, gates and trees recognize me. They probably saw all my shenanigans and laughed when I laughed, cried when I cried. And sure enough, did the same for thousands of people before me.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
It’s a very different way of life. Not easy to get used to in the beginning. But once you see past the struggles you’ve faced while coming here, you’ll appreciate that it’s a crazy time to be alive and a privilege to be taking the longer routes just because you can.

I’ve always been the kind of person to say – “Chahe koi jaye na jaye mai toh jaungi”. And that’s how I travelled this mini world on my Vector-91, one road at a time. If freedom was being offered so generously, why wouldn’t I take it?
Occasionally, my vector-91 had company and so did I 🙂

My first year, as I recall, was full of nightouts, random OAT jamming, exploring rooftops, new buildings and discovering corners of IITK I wasn’t supposed to be in (*cpi cries in one of these corners). I met some people I would never have met if fate hadn’t placed us together in the same hostel rooms or classrooms.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
Everyone comes from vastly different backgrounds, and ways of looking at the world. For the first time in my life, I had my differences with people. That’s when I learnt my first lesson at IITK.
Through shared experiences, we begin borrowing pieces of one another’s perspectives. Our differences don’t disappear, but some sort of ‘dissolution’ takes place.

The second Eureka moment caught me in the middle of a health crisis, when seasonal asthma and hospital visits became more frequent than I would’ve liked. I had friends who sat patiently with me, checked in on me multiple times a day and took care of me as nothing less than a family member. I remember being genuinely confused by it. What were they getting out of it? I never expected anything. It was the purest and most unconditional thing ever. That’s when I realised, you don’t always have to carry it alone, sometimes the real ones choose to carry it for you <3

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
The first year is usually the most chaotic. There were times when I didn’t like the place much, had trouble fitting in, and felt like I’m between a bunch of people who didn’t understand me or care about the things I care about. Somewhere between my exams, some turbulence in my personal life, a solo flight to Oman two days later (I was visiting family) and juggling 3 projects at once, I found myself sleep deprived, emotionally exhausted, but still going. I was doing my best to cope with the transition and aftermath of leaving first year circles behind.
First-year-me couldn’t cope with college but second-year-me kinda fought for it. There was a point I vividly remember when I was sitting in Naveen Tiwari’s fluids class, tears rolling down my face and I realised – the man in the shirt won’t stop teaching just because I’m not ready to understand and the world won’t stop spinning just because I’m having a bit of trouble. So… we keep going.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
This place taught me to begin without being ready, grind but also to take breaks, to celebrate and to grieve. It taught me to follow and break rules and to find those gaps (which I eventually covered ;)) in between when I could let things slide.
ICG gave me a new set of friends that I hung out with between projects and all-nighters. They saw the version of me that was growing, coming out of my shell, innocently taking up responsibilities and finding joy in my work. Inter-IIT Cult in second year December will remain one of my most cherished memories of college. More than the medal, it’s the endless BTS and memories I took back.
Even sem had me making some unexpected friends. And this is something I did till my last semester- made new friends. There’s no right time and you’re never too late or early for it. Some of those friendships I hold very dear to this day.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
It was around this time when ICS found me. Almost like I was walking a straight path but a small road emerged out of somewhere, inviting me to wander and I planned to take it. The road just grew wider with time and didn’t leave me till the last day no matter how hard I tried.
It felt like a new-found home, a breath of fresh air in my otherwise blehh life. The place gave me purpose and made me feel like I belonged. It introduced me to some of the strongest people I know, helped me make bonds so timeless – I’ll meet these people years later and I know nothing would change.
I spent my second year summers mostly with orientation and intern prep. So as ironic as it might sound, the summer was eventful but I had decided – I will go easy on myself.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
And so I did. Third year and this new found way of living had me exploring the freedom we talk about in IITs. Orientation was all about sleepless nights, but so much fun. From giving tests backstage with my team members to nightouts that had become routine, planning the logistics for the next day to watching people dead asleep in the office as if a storm had swept by.
On the last day, after Zumba we came back all sweaty, hungry, emotional from OAT, sat together at the office and jammed.
In the midst of some Anuv Jain song and my battle-worn teammates singing and crying, I realised- this is the whole point.
I didn’t have an intern by that time, 101 degrees fever that day but it didn’t matter. I was content.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
Fifth sem taught me how stressing out over things is literally pointless. A wise senior once said – “Nothing good ever came out of worrying”. He probably doesn’t even remember he said it but those of us who heard it do 🙂
I got an offer from SLB like a month into the internship drive, which was pretty late. But I had decided that fifth-sem-Harshita won’t stress out, will work hard but not at the cost of health or happiness.
I had a lot of new experiences that sem (such as being buzzed in the middle of exams :P). My girl group kept expanding meanwhile and things started becoming more wholesome. I was in a steady state.
I finally understood what makes the dream, the dream.
The sixth semester was full of good days. New friends, card games, badminton, impromptu plans and my first trip to Rajasthan with my newfound friend circle (shoutout to CL). Life felt full.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
But if I had to title that semester it would be- the higher the highs, the lower the lows.
I can confirm there will be times when it feels like you’ve lost your compass, when you aren’t anything close to what you thought you’d be, and when dragging yourself out of bed will be your biggest achievement of your day.
It is possible that you’ll try to set those goals over and over again and will fail.
To anyone going through an existential crisis in college, feeling like you don’t yet have it figured out and college is about to end in a year – it’s okay, trust me it is. Sometimes you just have to hold on tight for dear life while the storm passes. Not every struggle needs to make sense while you’re living through it.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
My internship helped me appreciate how there is a ‘real world’ out there and we confine ourselves so much to the happenings of college. Sometimes it serves as a friendly reminder that you aren’t just a secretary of a club or an X.Y cpi holder or a Coordinator or a Gensec. Yes, you hold those positions, but look a little beyond and we’re all just a bunch of kids in a big college in the country trying to make it out in the best way possible.
It might sound cliche coming from me (and I’m already prepared for the inevitable “ICS Coordinator” remarks that will come my way when I say this) but honestly and sincerely, try to see past the differences. Judge a little less. Talk to someone who looks like they’re struggling.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
Because I along with a lot of my batchmates can confirm it hurts like hell to walk past the door where you know someone couldn’t make it.
It’s painfully unsettling and the world doesn’t pause long enough for you to understand what happened.
I wish we didn’t get so consumed in these damn affairs. I wish we could be the kids we once were who found it so easy to make friends merely because we found another standing on the same ground as us, breathing the same air as us, eating in the same place as us.
I don’t believe we ought to guard ourselves so much, it comes at the cost of another.
As much as I love IIT Kanpur, I want to be brutally honest about its pain. My heart sinks, feels hollow to think of how many people around us are carrying battles we cannot see. How many are convinced they have to fight them alone. At the end of the day, none of the things we spend so much time worrying about feel important enough.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
And If you’re someone who’s struggling, I promise you life gives us multiple chances. An institute might not. A person might not. A professor might not. But life does. So.. Stay. One day you’ll be grateful you did.
—————————————————————————————-
I had my fair share of dilemmas. Some visible, some not. Up until my seventh semester I didn’t know if I should be pursuing a job, research or god knows what. For someone who liked having answers, that uncertainty was terrifying.
Prof Sanjay Mittal, after a candid conversation, made me realise that I must pick a path and go all in. Because if it’s not right, you can always choose again later.
Honestly man. Do what you enjoy. I literally took ukulele classes right during my ‘placement semester’ (played ‘When I’m gone’ a day before I left campus hehe). And I would quietly go and come without telling anyone because I enjoyed having a little time to explore music by myself. Thankfully, got a decent job – not just me but all my friends too. Yes, it’s the collective that counts.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
It’s been a wonder of a journey. I couldn’t vibe to the music during my first Antaragni. I remember standing there awkwardly, unnecessarily shy, as if I had somehow frozen in place while everyone else had a secret handbook on how to have fun. By my last Antaragni, I was dancing like nobody was watching (my friends can confirm that they definitely were). Somewhere between those two Antaragnis, I learnt how to enjoy myself a little more, worry a little less, and stop taking life so seriously.
Character development, I suppose.
If I entered IIT Kanpur with a rank and suitcases, I leave with something way harder to describe.
This place taught me life through a thousand of my ‘Eureka’ moments and oddly, none of them were scientific.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
What do I miss the most about college?
Not the big achievements or milestones, rather the little things.
By God’s grace, I never had to pick up an eyeliner on my own or drape a saree by myself because there were enough people to do it for me. How the littlest of things held so much meaning, and the most stressful times became a little easier to handle when one of us cracked a joke.
I highly recommend going on a girls trip if you get the chance. Peak life experience.
There is something special about finding ride-or-die people. The kind who will have your back no matter how rough things get. The kind with whom nothing is awkward and nothing is too absurd to say out loud. In fact, the weirder the question, the better the conversation.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
I’ll miss randomly barging into Roy’s room to hug her when I’ve had a rough day, Srishtis’s and mine roz ka “walk pe chalein?” and Shrasti breaking a leg (literally or metaphorically) every semester. Sneha’s room being the adda of our unhinged activities. Shriya being literally my TWIN in spirit and Sanya being the baby of our trips <3 (shout out to the lots of other people that i would like to mention, thank you for everything)
The funniest thing about friendship is that over time, you develop an entire language together. Inside jokes, and a sense of humour that makes no sense to others. Ours felt like it transcended all known physical planes of existence.
And now, my entire college support system is moving to Bangalore while I head to Mumbai.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
When they said, “Hum sab milke iski tickets karwayenge, isko Bangalore le aayenge,” I laughed and cried at the same time.
And if they forget, well. I’ll fly to BLR and simply show up at their doors whenever I feel like it.

*sigh*
I’m not ready to face the feeling that all of this has ended. How I’ll never be a student again, acceptably young and naive.
It felt like life threw me in a playground. So, I played and now it’s supper time. Except that I’m not going to play again tomorrow. And it doesn’t hit until tomorrow comes.
Very soon I’ll probably be sitting in an apartment in Mumbai at some odd night hours, thinking about how I could be getting a vanilla chocochip at naughty blenders, knocking on my wingies’ doors or playing Mafia with my gang. I will no longer be able to sit in the open lawns, looking at the various colours of the sky, or pick my study spots when I don’t feel like ccd-ing or library-ing. I’ll miss the professors whose classes I never attended, the notes I should’ve read, every plant in nursery and park 67, the sky walk mornings and OAT nights. I’ll miss the H1 backgate guard yelling at me across the street whenever I cycled down the Gupta road without holding the handles.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
I’ll miss clicking far too many pictures of the moon, the hall chhats and lying on the solar panels as if they were designed for me to stare at the stars. I’ll miss deciding to eat healthy every morning and ending up at Mama Mio the very same night. I’ll miss my ’bachhe’, the awfully funny moments, the parties and the elegant little jasmine we planted in the backyard. I’ll miss coming back from the library after an all-nighter and sleeping after breakfast. I’ll miss the “jaldi se jaake aate hain” doaa trips. Part of me wants to write it all down. Not because I’ll forget but because I feel afraid that the memories might seem so distant one day I’ll wonder if it really happened.

Written by: Harshita Chhuttani
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #76: Until Supper Time]
