=== As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On ===
Source: https://voxiitk.com/as-we-leave-77-leaving-the-porch-light-on/
Date: 2026-07-29

In this 77th edition of As We Leave 2026, Manya Gupta, a Y22 from the Department of Chemical Engineering, looks back on four memorable years shaped by friendships, shared experiences, and moments that turned IIT Kanpur into a home away from home. Through stories of laughter, milestones, and the people who made every phase special, she reminds us that the most cherished memories are often found in the simplest moments we share along the way.

Disclaimer: The views presented below are the author’s own and are not in any manner representative of the views of Vox Populi as a body or IIT Kanpur in general. This is an informal account of the author’s experiences at IITK.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
After procrastinating for a really long time and taking even longer to process that my time at IITK has actually ended, I have really tried my best to sum up my four years at IITK in this AWL.
When I first came here, I thought college would be about academics, placements, and somehow figuring out what I wanted to do with my life. Four years later, while all of those things did matter, they aren’t what I remember the most.
What I remember are the people. The random conversations, the late-night walks, the group meets, the fest nights, the endless rants, the setbacks, and all the little moments that somehow became my favourite memories.
It may not have been the most extraordinary journey, but it shaped me in more ways than I could have imagined. So before I leave, here’s my attempt at looking back on everything that made IITK what it was for me.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
The Beginning
Like every other fresher who comes to college hoping for a fresh start and a chance to make new friends, I was no different. Having spent most of my school life as an introvert, I had made up my mind that college would be different. I wanted to step out of my comfort zone and get to know as many people as I could. And honestly, orientation went pretty well. Between my IITK family, my wingies, and all the random interactions happening every day, I thought I was settling in nicely.
Then classes began. I still remember walking into class on the first day and feeling completely lost. Somehow, all the people I had met during orientation ended up in different groups. For the first time since arriving on campus, I felt truly alone. I was still figuring out the campus, trying to understand the academic system, and wondering whether I actually belonged here.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
Thankfully, that phase didn’t last very long. I soon found people with whom I could share all of this. Whenever I felt alienated in such a competitive environment, they reminded me that everyone was struggling in their own way. The transition to IITK wasn’t very smooth for me, but the people around me made it a lot easier.
It’s honestly funny how some of the most random encounters end up becoming such an important part of your life. One day you’re meeting someone for the most stupid reason possible and before you know it, they’re one of the people you’ll remember long after college ends.
First year also came with its fair share of surprises. Somehow, I managed to fracture my foot on the morning of my MTH111 endsem. How exactly that happened is a funny story in itself, but I’ll skip that part for now XD. Having barely spent a month in college, I had no clue what to do next. Missing out on events and not being able to walk properly brought a lot of FOMO.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
Thankfully, my roommate came to the rescue. Without a second thought, she was there through everything. I still remember her cycling me around campus so I wouldn’t miss out on hanging out with everyone, whether it was a late-night movie or just a random mama mio visit.
Even all my wingies were incredibly supportive. One would always bring me food from the mess, another would accompany me to all my doctor visits, and somehow everyone made sure I never felt like I was dealing with it alone. They made that whole phase so much easier. We spent most of our time together, usually sitting in one room and talking about the most random things. E1-113 eventually became our spot, and even today, just thinking about that room brings back so many memories. Honestly, I’m still trying to figure out how I got so lucky <3.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
Apart from all of this, I also had so many firsts. From attending concerts during fests for the first time to cycling to Ganga Barrage just to watch a sunrise, I became close to an amazing set of people(the “mujhe kya, main toh cute hu” group). Somehow, we were the most random mix of personalities, yet also the most compassionate. Random late-night walks, planning future trips, pulling all-nighters, movie nights in ESB; we did everything a fresher group does. Though, some of those memories only exist because Tamoghna and Zartaf had to call me multiple times whenever an “impromptu” plan was made and I am so glad they did :’)
And just like that, first year was over. It was only the beginning of a journey that still had a lot of surprises waiting for me.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
The Sophomore Year:
Following the IITK tradition, second year began with new additions to my family. Being on the other side of orientation was really my first step towards growing up. Interacting with such a diverse group of people and taking baccha wing meets taught me a lot about understanding people without any biases.
Academically, second year felt very different. The batch got split into branch courses and suddenly everyone wasn’t going through the same things together anymore. The coursework became heavier, labs started taking up a lot of time, and catching up with friends became harder than before as everyone got busier with their own commitments. Yet somehow, we always tried our best to spend maximum time together and also started to understand each other better.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
Alongside all this, I also got involved in the AnC Council. A council whose existence gets questioned every year XD, but one where I met some really dedicated people. I still remember the effort everyone put into organising intern and placement preparation programs so that at least some people could actually benefit from them. Somewhere along the way, CDev became an important part of my IITK journey, and I eventually decided to become a Manager there.
For a significant amount of time, I was really caught up on becoming a CTM. Like most people, it was something I had admired since day 1 of orientation. Many of my close friends were working towards it too and I thought it would be nice to get to spend even more time with them. But things didn’t work out the way I had hoped, and it took me some time to come to terms with it. I was scared that it would affect our friendship too but the way they handled the whole situation only made me appreciate them more.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
Now that I think about it, I think I spent way too much time stressing about things that didn’t matter as much as I thought they did. It always feels like everyone around you has everything figured out and you’re somehow falling behind. But over time, I realized that’s rarely true. Everyone is figuring things out at their own pace, and there’s no single path that works for everyone.
And before I knew it, even second year was coming to an end.

Intern Season:
The summer before intern season suddenly felt much more important than any summer before. While most of my friends stayed back on campus, I decided to go home. Unfortunately, that decision didn’t work out very well for me. I struggled to stay productive, felt mostly clueless about where to begin, and ended up not preparing well for any of the profiles.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
Somewhere along the way, I had convinced myself that Day 1 wasn’t meant for me. That lack of confidence probably hurt me more than anything else. Despite being eligible for so many tests, I had zero shortlists for day 1 even when I used to give the tests pretty much like everyone else.
It hit me harder than I expected. While most people around me were busy preparing for interviews, I was stuck wondering where things had gone wrong. On Day 1, I didn’t even feel like being around people. I remember going to the library just to get away from everything, only to realise that everyone there was talking about the exact same thing.
Those were probably some of the toughest days of college for me. There were times when I didn’t even feel like coming back to the hall or meeting anyone. I started thinking that this might even negatively affect my friendship and I would lose them as well but that really was not the case. They were all so supportive and understanding, be it the people who got sorted or the ones who were struggling just as me.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
Intern season has a very clear way of showing you who your people really are. From helping me prepare for tests and interviews to patiently listening to all my rants, my friends somehow managed to keep me going when I had almost given up. And so the cycle continued—tests, shortlists, rejections and just waiting.
This went on for almost the entire semester until the day before my endsem, when I got recruited by Vedanta. Finally getting my RAS blocked was a huge relief. By that point, I had become far too hopeless, and that one result gave me back some much-needed confidence.
At the same time, I knew from the beginning that Core wasn’t where I saw myself in the long run. So instead of treating it as the end of the journey, I considered it as a second chance, to learn from all the mistakes I had made and not repeat them during placements. And finally, a phase that had felt never-ending came to an end.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
We had a trip planned, a 4-day camping trek to Brahmatal for which a lot of convincing was required but all thanks to Vaneesha and Mansi who planned and managed everything. As physically exhausting as it was, especially for the “Bottom 3”, it was completely worth it. Being away from our phones and disconnected from everything else felt surprisingly refreshing. It was exactly the break all of us needed and it brought us even closer to each other.

Sixth Semester:
After the roller coaster that was intern season, sixth semester felt relatively chill. Life went back to complaining about 8 AM classes, making impromptu plans, cafe hopping, and simply finding time to hang out with friends. We really tried to make the most of that semester before life got serious again.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
Although the highlight of this semester was the fun hall 1 parties; all the clubs celebrating their end-tenures, seniors giving farewell parties, it was also one of the most bittersweet parts of college. Saying goodbye to our seniors; our ammas, bapus, club seniors, and so many others who had guided us ever since we arrived at campus. They had watched us grow through all our phases and had always been there whenever we needed them.
Watching them leave made everything feel a little too real. For the first time, it struck me that our turn wasn’t very far away either. Soon after, everyone left for their summer internships while I went to Udaipur. To be honest, the internship wasn’t exactly what I had imagined but it taught me a lot. More importantly, interacting with IITK alumni there made me truly appreciate the strength of the IITK network. They guided me throughout my project, helped me whenever I got stuck, and eventually, I was fortunate enough to secure a PPO as well.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
Placement Season:
Coming back from my internship motivated me to work even harder for the profile I actually wanted. This time, I knew I couldn’t afford to repeat the same mistakes. I was primarily targeting Analyst roles, with Product as a backup. I started reaching out to seniors relevant to my target profiles and followed their advice as closely as I could.
More than half of my wing wasn’t sitting for placements, and for the longest time I kept complaining about how isolated I felt in the process. Little did I know that it was going to turn out to be a blessing. Even though they weren’t going through it themselves, they were as involved in the process as if it were their own. From helping me with my resume and countless tests to eventually running around for my interviews, they gave their all.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
Around the same time, two of my friends were also targeting similar roles. As the process moved ahead, we started preparing together and that ended up being one of the best things that happened during placement season. Having people who were going through the exact same thing made it a bit easier. We discussed all our doubts, shared every small win and setback, and somehow kept each other motivated throughout.
August and September went by preparing as much as we could. By October, the pressure started feeling very real. Tests had begun, companies had started their processes, and placements no longer felt far away.
I still remember having a deck submission just a day after Diwali. Everyone was scattered across different places, yet they still spent hours helping me with it. A week later, I got my first shortlist. It might sound like a small thing, but after months of preparation and uncertainty, that shortlist meant a lot. For the first time in a long while, I felt that maybe all the effort was actually leading somewhere.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
November brought even more tests and with that, a lot of anxiety. The crazy thing about placement season is that you want it to end as quickly as possible, but as the final days approach, you suddenly feel like you need more time.
And before I knew it, there was only one day left. For me, it began with three shortlists on 1.1 and three on 1.2.
The D-Day
My first interview went surprisingly well and before I could even process how it had gone, my friends were already dragging me to the next one. The entire night became a blur of running between interviews and companies. At one point, everything seemed to be going according to plan, but by the end of 1.1, two really big opportunities had slipped away from me.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
And just like that, I had nothing. At around 5 AM, I walked out of Hall 13 feeling completely defeated. As I cycled back to my hall, it felt like intern season all over again. The same uncertainty and self-doubt, the same feeling that maybe things weren’t going to work out. I came back to my room, slept for barely two hours, and then it was time to start again.
Around 10 AM, one of my juniors called to ask whether I was participating in a company’s process. Half the process had already been completed. My heart sank because this was one of the companies I was heavily targeting and probably my best remaining chance. I rushed to Hall 13, and thanks to Ankit’s great convincing skills, I somehow managed to get a slot.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
After the first round, I was convinced this was the only opportunity I should focus on since I had no Day 2 shortlists. But my friends insisted I attend another company’s process as well. They helped me revise my resume, discuss my projects, and prepare for HR questions; all in a matter of minutes. I walked into that interview with almost no expectations.
A few hours later, I had offers from both companies. Even then, I was leaning towards the wrong choice. I still remember Harshita, Khushi, Moni, Mansi, Ankit circling around me, patiently talking through every factor until I could finally make an informed decision. The moment I accepted the offer, they had tears in their eyes. Honestly, so did I. It felt less like my achievement and more like something all of us had worked towards together.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
But the placement season doesn’t really end when you get placed. Not when your friends are still going through it. Day 1 and Day 2 were brutal. People barely slept, skipped meals, and kept showing up for every opportunity despite being exhausted. Watching everyone push through that phase with so much patience was honestly something to learn from.
Finally, by the end of Day 3, all of us were placed. That moment was one of the most wholesome feelings I’ve experienced here. After months of preparation, stress, and uncertainty, everyone around me finally looked relieved.

The Final Show
The most awaited semester had finally arrived. Suddenly, all those plans that had been living in our heads for years had an actual deadline. We knew this was our last chance to make the most of the freedom IITK had given us, so saying no to anything simply wasn’t an option anymore. In fact, we even made a bucket list of everything we wanted to do before leaving campus.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
It started with frequent cooking sessions, trying to experiment with what we could make using an induction. Then came our girls’ nights; making air clay art, baking cupcakes, doing Just Dance, watching movies on the projector, singing our hearts out during karaoke, painting canvases, and literally just saying yes to every random idea someone came up with. We even became surprisingly consistent with badminton. We were definitely not good at it, but that was never the point. Looking back, those evenings of laughing at each other’s terrible shots are probably what I’ll remember the most.

This semester was also full of travel. It began with a short trip to Lucknow followed by an unforgettable trip to Meghalaya where we somehow agreed to do the craziest things (reaching the infinity pool and cliff jumping being some of them XD). After all that, a peaceful trip to Landour, enjoying the views and hopping between beautiful little cafes. Every trip had its own vibe, and every one of them gave us stories we’ll always remember.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
As graduation started feeling real, there was this constant urge to capture everything before it all became a memory.  We made so many cringe reels, all for a tiny 3–4 second clip in the batch video. Spending three to four hours planning, shooting and reshooting those few seconds sounds absolutely insane, but it was all worth it.
And then came all the “lasts.” The branch farewell, the batch photoshoot, realizing that these were the final pictures we’d ever click together as a class. Just as that was over, DE practices had begun. H6 had never felt more lively; every balcony, every terrace and every open space had someone practicing. Even people who had never danced before showed up and gave it everything they had. Seeing the whole batch come alive during those few weeks made the final performances even more fun.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
The last week on campus was probably the most happening one. There was something planned almost every day. H1 ki Baarat, Scribble Day, Abhinandan; few last events for the entire batch to come together. Everyone laughed, danced, clicked endless photos and tried to make the most of every moment. Our wing even painted a picture of the eight of us on the C3 wall, leaving behind something that would stay even after we left.
And then, just like that, it was time to leave.
The goodbyes were easily the hardest part. After spending four years together, it was strange to think that this wouldn’t be our everyday anymore. Somewhere between all the lectures, hostel nights, random plans and endless conversations, IITK had quietly become home. Leaving the campus was one thing, but leaving the people behind was something none of us were really prepared for.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
Looking Back
Four years later, I realised that college was never just about finding the right club, getting good grades, or eventually landing a job.
It was about the people who celebrated every small win and stood beside me during every setback. It was the little moments that never seemed important at the time but somehow became the ones I’ll remember the most; walking aimlessly around campus with friends, the whole wing sitting in one room for hours doing absolutely nothing, or last-minute plans that somehow turned into the best memories.
I came here as someone who was unsure of herself, constantly questioning whether she belonged. Somewhere along the way, through all the mistakes, opportunities and friendships, I became a little more confident in myself. This journey was far from perfect, but I don’t think I’d want it any other way.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #77: Leaving the Porch Light On]
Thank you, IITK- for the people, the memories, and for giving me a place that will always feel a little like home <3.

Written by: Manya Gupta
Edited by: Adiba Areej Laskar, Lavanya Srivastava

=== As We Leave #78: To V or not to V ===
Source: https://voxiitk.com/as-we-leave-78-to-v-or-not-to-v/
Date: 2026-07-30

In the 78th edition of As We Leave 2026, Aditya a Y22 student from the Department of Statistics and Data Science, reflects on a transformative journey of discovering identity, embracing ambition, and finding belonging at IIT Kanpur. Through stories of friendship, campus communities, creative pursuits, hard-earned dreams, and moments of introspection, he explores how college is less about becoming someone new and more about understanding who you already are. Ultimately, his story is a heartfelt tribute to the people and communities that turned a campus into home, leaving him with memories, purpose, and a deeper sense of self.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
PROLOGUE
I always knew I’d write an AWL. When I was a CGM, I worked on an AWL that was quite bland and read like a written CV. My first and foremost hope, therefore, is that Dishita has more fun working on this one than I did when I was in her shoes :). But now that I’m on the other end, it’s a tricky line to tread. Dear reader, you are at most 4 years younger than me. I do not have nearly as much wisdom to offer you as I thought I would by this point. But I will try my best to ensure you walk out of this with something to smile about and, hopefully, something to think about.
Once upon a time in a distant land, there lived a little rabbit. The rabbit had lived under a rock for two years while the world outside was ravaged by a storm, the likes of which the world had never known, and the rabbit had all the time in the world to think. And so he thought. And he thought

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
The rabbit thought quite a lot about himself because he really is all he had to think about. And soon the rabbit thought he knew all there was to know about himself, and he believed this core would never be shaken.

And thus, as the storms subsided, the rabbit set out into the world, upon the Everglades that called to all rabbits that turned of age. The Everglades were a strange place. Full of possibility and potential. Rabbits of all kinds showed up to the Everglades. And our little rabbit, let’s call him P, felt quite overwhelmed.
Under his little rock, life was all about him, but here? Life was about everything but him. Our little P did not like that one bit. So he set about the Everglades, trying to carve out a piece that would be his and his alone.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
The following is by far not all I have to say, but I don’t think I’ll ever be able to achieve that. It is at the very least true. And raw. I hope you enjoy.
P.S. Shoutout to my sister for the title.
ACT I—the Discovery
P felt quite taken aback by this new land. The other rabbits there spoke differently. They laughed at different things, and they did things differently. They were rabbits, yes, all, at the end of the day, and yet he felt so far away.
He did not know if he would find his own fluffle. He drifted from group to group, seeking to find rabbits that would claim him as their own, and he would attempt to claim other rabbits as his.
It was not easy, and it was daunting at times. But the rabbit knew he did not want other rabbits that did just as the ones at home did. The rabbit wanted to grow, and to grow, he needed to see beyond the little rock he grew up under.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
And soon he found them.

Picture this. A freshly legal kid, with eyes full of wonder, a stomach full of flu, and a head barely full of hair. I have no energy at this point. The sun has sucked out anything the flu left behind. And the wonderful room services of the very grand hotel celebration have left me incapable of physical recovery.
I stumble to my room, seeking shelter from the heat and a moment’s respite from the terribly long room key-receiving process. Dear reader, I knew Hindi very well. I’d learned it for 10 years after all. But oh, imagine my surprise at finding out the general populace does not speak as they do in the textbooks. “Dhoop toh bohot hai,” I believe, were my first words to Suneet Maharana’s father, words that immediately made Suneet decide I was to be bullied.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
This was a momentary reaction, of course; most of any clout I had in this institute in those first months came from Suneet and Vignesh regaling their very large social circles with tales of my supposed rap talent (talent that I quite superbly showcased at the fresher’s night performance. Iykyk)
Suffices to say, the learning curve was not easy. I found myself constantly seeking out the others from Bangalore and the south to try and speak as myself, and not a character I was forced to adopt every time I spoke Hindi.
Dear reader, anyone in my batch will tell you I’m the funniest guy around. (No, really). But in Hindi? Language is a huge part of your personality. It was humbling to learn that awkward and charismatic (or at least slightly less awkward) are two sides of the same coin, a coin that flips every time you switch languages.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
Getting comfortable with a newer language is hard. Being able to speak it and still be interesting is even harder. It’s a journey for anyone who comes from the south. But if anyone wants my advice, the effort is worth it. The right people will stick by you and hear you fail time and time again, and the laughs they let go of at the very shitty jokes you have managed to come up with are what help you learn eventually. The right people will wait for you to find yourself. And every time you forget, they will be there to help you remember, because sometimes they know you better than you know yourself.
Act II—the hair.
P had a sick coat. A coat that he tended to with pride. It was unruly at first. Hard to look at. Awkwardly shaped and insufficiently maintained.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
But with time, it became something truly extraordinary. Or so he keeps telling himself.

My hair has changed more than me through the years. I’ve looked like a tennis ball; I’ve looked like Willy Wonka. I’ve looked like Dora the Explorer, and I’ve (not my words) looked like Eren Yeager. I’ve looked like Jesus Christ, and I’ve looked like a gay samurai.
But regardless, what matters are those little moments when the women in your life tell you they want your hair, and you realize you have inadvertently achieved something of value. Something that makes the sis guards stop your friends and ask them why you didn’t do entry in your own hall. Something that makes dudes mistakenly check you out for a wee minute in the gym and then get angry at themselves when you turn around. Something that makes barbers refuse to cut your hair because they want you to realize its full potential (and then donate it to cancer, as one suggested).
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
Its not always been this long, though. I came to this college with short hair, like everyone else. Very short, in fact. The kinda hair that bristles when you run your hand through it.
But what was a skinny kid with meh hair and a geeky appearance to do when he comes to a university and decides he wants to better himself. The gym was too much work. The face was too expensive to touch. Clothes required shopping and taste. All you really had was the hair.
Now, long hair is not a look I’ve ever frowned upon. Being relatively fond of rock and metal, the look always had its appeal, but I never thought I’d be one to adopt it.
A wolf cut gone wrong is what started this all.
The hair was never meant to be this. But it is what it is now.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
I’ve pondered cutting it off many a time. It’s not a look I’m fond of all the time, either. But having been the kind of kid that gets lost in a crowd, whom you wouldn’t really look at twice, all my life, the hair makes me stand out. Very rarely do I meet someone who does not remember me.
It’s a welcome change.
I’d rather know I’ve left a mark, even at the risk of turning some people off.
I suppose that says a lot about me.
I think many people in college have their own “hair”: a new hobby you may have picked up, a skill you acquired, or a personality trait you adopted. Anything you changed about yourself, until it was more you than the rest of you. An identity trait that you hold onto tightly because you doubt what you’d be to yourself and to people without it.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
Reader, I have this very stupid saying that I made up and tell people a lot.
“You do because you are; you are not because you do.”
You can always cut your hair. It’s not what makes you you. You are what made the hair. And be sure people will know that. And you will keep making that splash you wanted to make.
P.s. Also, headbanging at parties is always fun.
I will probably donate my hair at some point; it’s only a matter of time. DM me, and I’ll give you a good rate.
Act III – Campus <3
Before he knew it, P was somebody people knew. He had carved out a piece of this new land for himself. He had found people he could call his own and places he felt he belonged to.
Before he knew it, the stories he had to tell were no longer stories from the rock; they were stories from the Everglades.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
Before he knew it, this was home.

During my second year of college, I went to the Inter-IIT Cult Meet 6.0. On the train ride there, we had the remarkable idea to prank the IITBHU fashion team (shout-out to Vidit Parmar, Abhigyan Das, and the man of the hour, Abhishek Sahu).
Posing as JEE aspirants on a boys’ trip to Gaya Junction (don’t ask), we began to shower them with awe and appraisal, seeking advice for the upcoming JEE mains exam, which they revealed to us was in less than a month’s time. Nevertheless, we managed to avert suspicion until Abhishek Sahu decided to tease a fourth-year about his placements, rampwalk down the corridor, fling off the towel he had around him, and reveal his IITK film club hoodie. He then taunted them for being from a “lower IIT” and ran down the corridor, while the rest of us stood there trying to figure out how to get out of here without escalating matters further.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
Soon, they were walking down the length of the train, searching for us and attempting to intimidate us into giving up Abhishek, as we sought asylum amongst the dance team.
Within minutes, however, we saw 5 coaches of IITK folks swarm down the train until the IITBHU fashion team was pushed back towards the train bathroom, with our then gensec at the front looking into the camera they had pulled out saying, “ham bas baat karne aaye hai”—as some lovely folks from IITK held up hockey sticks in the background.
On a more serious note, there have been three campus-wide protests during my time here. In all, I saw people band together as one. Some just for the sake of it, some out of concern, and some out of selfishness, but all showing up for another.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
This campus bolsters communal bonds with collective purpose in a way I have never seen before and imagine I never will.
Friendships are born through circumstance. Through turmoil that you’re thrown into together. Collective trauma. Group enjoyment. Common hatred. And the opportunity to band together for more than yourself.
Beyond anything else, this campus provides the most unique blend of elements that form, in a very short time span, the most meaningful adult friendships one could hope to build.
The campus infrastructure is such that every week there is an opportunity to achieve, and where there is an opportunity to achieve, there is an opportunity to fail and seek support, to win and celebrate, or to sit back and bitch.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
Every month, there are new people met, each going through dynamically different social and emotional changes in an academically charged and competitive environment – a volatile cocktail that explodes relationships just as the fragments of broken ones shower others with strength.
College is the most ambitious social experiment you will ever participate in, the reward being the truest and most finely filtered friends you could hope to find.
I am leaving this college with 0 burnt bridges. I do not know if that is a good or a bad thing. But at least I know that through all the ups and downs, there is no one I haven’t spent a meaningful moment with whom I wouldn’t pick up the phone for and who wouldn’t do the same for me.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
Act IV – Dreams and regrets
P dreamt large, and P believed he was meant for greatness. He, however, was far from perfect.
You see, back where he came from, he never had enough to fear loss. A decision made in haste, or an opportunity lost, meant little more than lost time and effort and, worst of all, a bruised ego. But now decisions had weight. He suddenly had things to lose.
P was a child no longer. And overthinker that he was, every time he lost, his thoughts ate him up alive.

I came to college with a single dream in mind. To eventually do a master’s in one of the world’s best universities. That dream faltered, developed nuance, got questioned time and time again, and changed in form.
But at the end of college, I still had broadly the same dream. Yeah, I wanted to do a PhD now instead, a dream I unfortunately fell short on (at least at the moment), but it’s something remarkable to know that if first year you saw where you were now, he’d have tears in his eyes.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
That’s why getting that offer of admission meant a lot to me.
It is hard to put into context how much that corrected in my head, but I will try to do so regardless.
During my intern season, I chose to have an academic preference. I was convinced all of this was just a backup, and I really did not care what sort of an internship I landed this season, and I did not conduct much research into what sort of pay or perks most companies offered.
I had a choice between two companies, a choice which to most would have been obvious. But through a combination of the wrong set of people and advice to receive in those crucial moments, a lack of awareness, absolutely terrible management, a lack of sleep, and a ton of last-minute overthinking from my end, I ended up picking a choice that, to me, made absolute sense until several months later.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
But as the political climate of the West changed and the possibility of getting a good grad offer seemed to dwindle, I came to regret giving up what would have been a very cushy couple of years before I tried again. This then led to months of replaying that moment and wishing things were different.
So when, after multiple rejections, I saw that acceptance from ETH Zurich, it wiped away months of regret and gave me, after a long time, true and genuine excitement.
My last couple of months at IITK were not a smooth sailing. My little group of friends and I, as individuals, went through a lot. But through it all, it was a hope and a sense of ambition that had rekindled after long, and my very patient, absolutely brutal and yet fiercely loyal group of friends that make me look back on this last year with nothing but a smile on my face and a tear in my eye.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
Dear reader, it is not easy to walk down one road and wonder what life would have been like on the other. But there is no such thing as a life spent without regrets. A “what-if” is inevitable if you ever find yourself at a meaningful crossroads. And the ability to be grateful that you even had the opportunity to stand at that fork and make a decision is something that is acquired with great effort –  an effort I still make to this day.
Act V – A community
P had always been a part of a family. But this time things were a little different. This time it was a family that really had no worldly force, no blood ties pulling them together. Anyone could leave. Anytime they wanted. And yet they stayed.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
P found a new family. Many new families. Families that came to be because it’s members chose each other.

When I was a secretary, Mutasim summoned us all to the OAT with a master plan. In hushed whispers and glances of shared excitement, a plot was hatched.
Shortly later, Anubhav came running, clearly tensed and angry, eyes darting, looking for Mutasim. His rage and worry were palpable and quite understandable.
What Debsoc coordinator wouldn’t be scared? If he were told that various problematic substances had been discovered in the Litsoc room after the Debsoc post-PD party, and that the coordinators had been summoned for questioning.
Anubhav’s wide eyes widened further, but his worry melted into relief and joy when he noticed not a lonely, worried Mutasim, but all of Debsoc IITK, standing before the chocolate room.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
“Happy Birthday!” We all shouted, embracing Anubhav and rubbing his face with cake, as Kunaal, Mutasim, and Sampada looked upon the crowd, broad smiles on their faces.
It was just a little moment, nothing extraordinary, but as I looked around me, I realized that I wasn’t in this place just to debate, or organize events, or become a coordinator. I was here for the people. I was here for the friends this place gave me and for the opportunity to give back to the place that made me so much more. I was here for my community, a family away from home.
It was this same feeling of kinship I felt when I was at THC, a ragtag team of hip-hop enthusiasts banded together by two of the most passionate seniors I have ever met in college—Vijay Bharadwaj and Shivang Jaiswal. They created something that I could only have ever wished to experience in college. To be able to put out a song for an event that our university hosted with such pride, to make music among people with so much passion it inspired mine, and to build memories with friends through it all was a privilege I am extremely grateful to have been given. And none of it would have been possible without community.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
Now you might ask me, “What is community?”
Well, reader, that is a question that I have answered formally twice in my DebSoc tenure, asked of others a lot more times, and pondered myself for a countless moments. It is a question I still don’t fully have the answer to. However, I have graduated and will not spend more time pondering the abstract; this question is for the 25s and 26s to think about now. Can’t have them copying this AWL to cheese their interviews, after all, can I?
Community is not really complicated to understand, though. It is something taken for granted until it falls apart, and it is what holds any body together.
It is the warm fuzziness that fills my heart when I look upon my juniors and batchmates drinking shakes at Mama Mio, arguing about the debate that just took place, and trying their level best to stop the conversation from digressing into 10 competing, equally degenerate topics.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
It’s the feeling of pride I get when I am in a cypher, during Antaragni or at the OAT, rapping my ass off without a care in the world, or on stage during Overdose or orientation.
It is the ambition I felt looking at the CGMs burst forth with energy and enthusiasm for the ideas they brought forth to a Vox meeting.
It is the joy I felt when Ishi and Nandini finally got the ELS site up and running and put up my proudest work, “Prisoners of Fate,” on it.
It is in support given and taken, moments wasted in unison, and laughter shared.
It is in shared tears and celebration.
It is in friendships born and continued.
It is the very soul that made Debsoc home, the collective ambition that helped THC survive and thrive, the curiosity that brought people to ELS, and the passion that made Vox do what it does.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
That, dear reader, is community. And if you are a junior of mine, be sure these same words uttered will still get you reduced to tears in any upcoming interview you give at Debsoc, so good luck.
Act VI—love

I wonder what it means to love
Does it feel like a calming breeze?
Does it feel like the cool wind that blows
When the summer ups and leaves
Does it look like the break of dawn
Peeking through the curtain night
Painting winding streaks of orange
On a sheet of sunshine bright
Does it sound like the patter of raindrops
Falling out a gloomy sky
Or does it sound like the final breath
of life well-lived’s last sigh
I often wonder what it means to love
To hold close as can be
To hold tight and hold dear
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
Till they’re a part of me
I wonder what it means
To rip apart your soul
And give it in an open palm
And yet feel so whole
To be loved is a gift or a curse
Or a gamble all the same
But to love and lose or lose on love
Rejoice if love has known your name
No, reader, you will not get gossip about me in this chapter. This isn’t about that kind of love, though it very well could be.
It’s an honor to be loved by a love earned. To have people choose to be with you and near you.

In a campus this large, whether it’s by chance, by careful determinism, or just sheer desperation, there are people who take time out of their lives to come see you. To ask you about yourself and to give the smallest of shits about you. That is love earned.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
It is important to remind yourself that one of your life’s greatest achievements is the people you’ve managed to keep in it. This thought has mammothed as I go through these final stages. I’m not someone who holds on very tightly to the people in my life. People drift in and out all the time, and I try not to pay mind. But to the few who chose to stick around, even if it was for a leg of the journey and not the whole way – know I felt seen. I saw you. And the time we have spent has meant something.
To my friends at Viral Hate Club and the clubs I have been in, to bhakbsdk, to bepagaarz. To the solo friendships I happened to form, to friendships that broke and reformed, and most importantly, to the guys who made college feel like college—Gupil, Zehaan, Ritesh, Abhigyan, Prabhu, Devansh (x2), Suneet, Pranav, Maharaajan, Animesh, Manasvi and Pranshu – y’all are the reason I started calling home home.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
Act VII – A goodbye
And just as he came. One day he left. And the actions of today became the memories of tomorrow.

Someone leaves.
One cries because they miss the little things they can no longer do with them.
The other cries because they know they are not very close and may possibly never see them again.
And the onlookers cry seeing tears being shed, knowing them who is leaving was loved.
I’m not an easy man to make cry. I grow numb when things get heavy. I feel by overthinking. And I release through poetry and thought. I left on the last possible day, and I saw almost everybody leave. My last moment on campus was in shared silence and mutual encouragement between me, Abhigyan Das, Prabhu Safi, and my family, who waited in the car. As such, my last few days in college were not sad. They were heavy. They were final. And they were romanticized in the best possible way. Each goodbye was not a bond broken but a celebration
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
of all that was built on this campus. It was souls that intertwined, only grazingly or holding tight, rising above what it meant within the confines of a campus.
Reader, if you know me, you are very familiar with my god complex. The legend of “The V.” A falsehood of confidence that has gotten me through many a tough spot in college. An obsession with myself that has driven my friends crazy to no end. In fact, if you are a member of the WhatsApp group titled “Fanbase of the V” (shoutout the prophet, Apoorv Tandon), then congratulations, you were one of the first to witness my narcissism in full bloom. Yeah, and for those who care, I am the fucker friend V.
And so it is with no regrets whatsoever that I am glad I left college with the veil firmly off.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
Yeah, fuck Kanye West, but he did give me the opportunity to leave college as I came.
Humble. With not an ounce of grandeur, pomp or event. With the family I was given and the family I made.
I got to see all my friends leave, and I got to feel the weight their absence left on me.
I got to tread the bones of a barren campus and realize the life that the people breathe into it.
IITK is a fallen corpse without us in it. And even as the Y22 batch left, I felt a piece of its soul exit and leave into the world, surely to be replaced by another. I am glad I was able to mourn you fully.
I will miss you, IITK. And I will miss making you you as you made me me.
Thank you for the opportunities you gave me, the people you helped me meet, the shitstorms you have put me through, and the pride and confidence you helped me build.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
Thank you for the stories you gave me to tell, the character you helped me shape, the decisions you gave me to make and the places you showed me.
Thank you for tolerating my presence and building me into so much more than I was.
And thank you for taking it easy on me.
EPILOGUE
When I came to college, I thought it would fix everything wrong about me. Through trials and tribulations I thought I would come out strong, independent, organized and completely put-together – well-equipped to take on the world. Dear reader, in humility I know I have a long way to go.
Old habits die hard, patterns repeat, we seek the familiar and it is hard to let go of what we used to be.
And the idea that you lived life so imperfectly that you now desire change is undesirable.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
To all the people I hurt in imperfection, intentionally or inadvertently, I apologize from the depths of my heart. And I hope you will remember me as fondly as I will remember you.
College did not fix me but it shone a light on what’s wrong. These four years have been a scathing analysis of every place I shine at, all the things I should be proud of and all the things I fall so terribly short at.
Perhaps that awareness is the greatest thing college could give you. The rest is upto you. Nothing can change you, that is on you and you alone.
But an awareness realized in good time, and understood, is a gift.
I leave this place with a future to look forward to, with people that I will know and love for life and with an awareness that I could have only hoped to achieve.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #78: To V or not to V]
And that is what I take with me, as I leave.
Written by: Aditya V
