=== As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction ===
Source: https://voxiitk.com/as-we-leave-79-uncanny-paths-to-one-direction/
Date: 2026-07-30

In this 79th edition of As We Leave 2026, Yash Giri, a Y22 from the Department of Mechanical Engineering, looks back on four memorable years shaped by executive leadership, hall culture, and deep-rooted service that turned IIT Kanpur into a home away from home. Through stories of navigating the Students’ Gymkhana, Senate dynamics, and life in Hall 5 and Hall 1, he reminds us that true fulfillment lies not just in savoring the campus experience, but in giving back to the community that helps you grow along the way.

Disclaimer: The views presented below are the author’s own and are not in any manner representative of the views of Vox Populi as a body or IIT Kanpur in general. This is an informal account of the author’s experiences at IITK.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
To all the readers,

If you’re here, it barely matters now if we ever cṛossed paths. My tumultuous yet joyful experience is now for you to breathe and take lessons – not to repeat my oopsies but to carve your own. Years of batches gracing this motherland and eventually leaving, all with their own uncanny tales of euphoria and plight, and this AWL captures some of those in my delightfully questionable stint.

With a myriad of feelings this heritage soil gave me, cherishing music was arguably the seminal one, and this piece is yet another musical for me, whose tunes took years to unfold, but I ultimately hope it is heard and revered by many.

Chapter 1: Live While We’re Young

“Yeah, we’ll be doing what we do
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
Just pretending that we’re cool, so tonight
Let’s go crazy, crazy, crazy ’til we see the sun”

The clock rolls back to 28th October, 2022; I arrived here with an ongoing battle with dengue. Indeed, my father was delighted with how ICS arranged the grand arrival of the inaugural post-COVID fully offline (& the greatest) batch – the Y22s.

Every air at each campus spot felt new, every opportunity seemed worth pursuing, and everyone seemed talented. With cloudy ambitions and almost zero clue about the “what” and “how” of IITK, I established a few basic rules for myself:

1) Try to meet as many new people. Embrace differences and learn to Respect.
2) If you doubt it, try it…..Enjoy all the damn endeavors that come along.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
3) Live while you’re young !

My academic journey couldn’t convince me to be included here. However, the Orientation exposed us to this very intriguing, exciting, and low-key super cool thing called the Students’ Gymkhana.

With a promise of “Enthu” as the only pre-req, it was akin to a mini-Institute with a plethora of opportunities and fun, and man, Everything was equally attractive.

As it turned out, that one MnC Club piqued my interest, something I eventually joined and soon left 🙁 but made my mark in it, which lasted as my forever impression across the years – the Humour House and its Snoop Dogg….My chains and shades justified the same later.

Since my arrogant soul longed for a solo stage performance, the selection wasn’t a cakewalk lol. I was dispatched to the OAT Top and 10 divergent voices (Y21s + Y20s) thundered…”Abh Khol” …Took 3-4 attempts but eventually did it flawlessly. Undeniably thrilling, but I learned nothing is going to come for free in this Institute.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
Yet again, I don’t wish to talk much about the first-quizzes and Mid-sem but Umm, you can see this:

Should we investigate the happening part of my first year – the General Elections (Aren’t they always very happening for me… iykyk)? So let’s not!
Galaxy, Freshers Inferno, and Takneek – participated with a podium finish in all But….

Did I actually follow my Rule 3? Did I Live While I Was Young?

Without a doubt !

I treated IIT Kanpur like an all-you-can-experience buffet with clubs, events, competitions, and performances. Guys, I sampled everything on the menu. Lived it, loved it. But then came the uncomfortable question:

Who benefited from all this? Me ?
Well… mostly me.

And therein lay the problem.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
All this time, I had somehow confined my efforts to the noble cause of savouring my own IITK experience. Beyond my little universe existed an entire IIT Kanpur fraternity that I scarcely paused to think about.

The realization was shameful: I loved the idea of service far more than the act of it.

And I could not unsee it.

Living for myself had been exciting. Living for something larger felt necessary.
Whilst the first baby steps were unclear, how spectacular is the IITK Freshers algorithm that makes everyone voyage the same ship but with a multitude of different waves and weather, leading to different shores. Desirable or not, that ship was docked, and I was en route to set out on a new quest – the President’s Office !

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
Chapter 2 - One Thing

I don’t, I don’t, don’t know what it is
But I need that one thing
And you’ve got that one thing

At the dusk of this chapter, let’s decipher together – that One Thing.

Well, the work kicked off, and we raced through initiatives with relentless enthu. Within weeks, I discovered the peculiar agony of an executive. The invisible hours, the endless expectations, and the constant pressure to deliver. Thought about Dhwanit—he got a life, too. Well, he didn’t agree.

Leadership, as I learned, looked like authority from a distance and accountability from up close.

Having toiled with substantial sweat for my pool in GC, shifting to the parent hall felt like a return to the roots, to our identity, and to the long-lived legacy whose first glimpse came when we noticed Dr. K.Vijayraghavan (Hall 5 alum, Google later) clicking pictures outside his room in F-bot, Hall 5. Seasons passed, and F-110 became the epicenter of all madness.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
F-110: the unofficial focal point of the incoming H5 HEC, the strategy HQ, the courtroom for petty fights, the airport-like common smoke room, the dreaded room for Y23s, the store room for Scratch Apparels, but finally, my abode – my motherly shelter and my nest for chaos.

Amidst our continued disappointments in GC events beneath that roof, I never realized when and how – mere H5 hall mates became my family, an inseparable force in me, and a source of unending happiness for me. They’re all famous (or infamous) enough to not introduce them. Hall 5 was no longer just an abode; it was soil we aimed to contribute to and discover bonds in its bustling walls, which shall echo for generations to come.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
However, the serene days of IITK’s Fall semester gradually began to dwindle into long hours at the DoSA Office, Senate Hall, and SAC. We poured ourselves into work, driven by an invisible force that seemed to whisper constantly from within. Don’t know what it was, but with every task completed and every initiative taking shape, I found myself cherishing my time at the President’s Office. I had begun to enjoy the simple act of getting things done.

9th February, 2024 — I lost a major election for the position of Parliamentarian, Students’ Senate. Perhaps that path was never meant for me, but the defeat stung more than I had expected. Tears, after years.

Yet, that very election gifted me Ramswaroop Bhaiya. Through conversations on research, astronomy, IISERs, and Senate affairs, he lovingly steered my wandering soul towards research opportunities that transformed my journey. Even today, I am still astounded and speechless as to how he left us. He will forever remain my first guide, my bhaiya and his demeanor will be ever-inspiring.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
The Manifesto, as I always mused, is a sacred promise you make to your people, and I vowed to get all those promises done. Yet I couldn’t do it all, but people benefited, and I was close to the answer.

It was Janhvi (Y20, Gender Cell Nominee) who trusted me with the responsibility of the Gender Cell. Somewhere along the way, I finally found the answer to that One Thing.

It was Campus Upliftment.

I spent months working on it before realizing I had fallen in love with it. For the first time, joy came not from achievement, but from making life a little better for someone else.

Chapter 3 - Perfect

If you like to do whatever you’ve been dreamin’ about
Then, baby, you’re perfect

The third year was perhaps the closest this place came to feeling perfect.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
Kicked off with a decent Intern on Day 1, and more profoundly, I found families, not one but plenty. I-mid became the first. It remained infamous for obvious reasons.

Through the Gender Cell and the Students’ Senate, I was graced with opportunities to add meaningful bricks. Days finally started to carry purpose, and I felt less useless. On some days, life was a Cadillac driving on Route 66 Motel, while on most, it was racing on the Monaco Circuit with no pit stop. Life was busy, chaotic, and henceforth Perfect.

Takneek’24 is still deeply etched in my brain cells, as Ayan made me the Chief Astronaut and handed over all Astronomy PSs as if by instinct. My crew didn’t disappoint and delivered.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
How could everything be so perfect? Where was disappointment, and where was agony ? It’s nearly unfathomable for me to comprehend, but I soon realized the hard truth—everything I am living now is barely persistent and so short-lived. Once the 4th year unfolds, 221218 will again become useless, and the path to continue taking responsibilities will come with costs I thought I was ready to bear. Later did I realize, nobody can.

The GC think tank did its job, and I continued my imperfectly perfect life. My mind and temperament in Senate hall began to fire up – I asked the tough questions and eventually chose that path—the lost candidate in Parli elections contested for Chair, SS….Who’d believe in him ?
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
Well, his friends did, his seniors did, his juniors did, the House did, and the Senate did.

Perhaps, Senate101 is a lore for my 10th Reunion in 2036, but this is exactly when every ounce of my body changed, and this time, the vow was not to some, but to all – everyone who is called a GBM here !

24th April, 2025 – The new Students’ Senate took Charge!

Chapter 4: Night Changes

“Everything that you’ve ever dreamed of , disappearing when you wake up.”

It is famously said that your final year is a mosaic of grind and glee, with placements looming over your head and the eventual heroics we do in our preparations. Mine was a story with thorns & scars, shouldered with the three Ps—Placement, Padhai, and Position…Oscillating between these unstable equilibria taught perseverance; however, I was losing the treasure I had carried with me all along – the time with my friends.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
Hall 1—it needs no drumroll, for it is the greatest force of character development across the Institute. Music Volume ?? You bet! No wing decides to back off. Random noises, our busy canteen, tranquil bird-watching from balconies in monsoon, and certainly, the birthplace for greatness. Those who lived here, those who will, are entitled to script history. No offence to other halls.
Nights changed forever, and the final chapter was about to conclude. Rightly so, Everything I ever dreamed of—the fun, the sports, the frisbee games, and my flute – disappeared with time. Perhaps, an inverted rainbow, unlike most.

8th Sem – Last chilling winters of KNP, and with every week that passed, the challenges grew scarier. Grind became a daily routine; where was glee? Where, even in the 8th sem?  The responsibilities and the legacy of B201 cannot be underestimated. But, I realised, you are free to choose b/w – Get busy living or Get Busy dying.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
Every Senate meeting that I chaired, every initiative that we crafted, every Senator whom I met, every individual whom I talked to, and everyone who appreciated or criticised – this AWL presents nothing but a debt of gratitude to you. All this time, I never realized how every single interaction made me light-years better.

You, my friend, are the truest inheritance of your institution. And you must stand up for yourself, for your people, for your hall, for your pool, for your rights, for your institute, and for your country. You can do it whilst having the best fun of your life.

Some undeniable facts you cannot debate:

Campus D-Shop deserves a separate fanbase. What a coffee!

Mama Mio is a legendary place after 12:00 AM

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
Campus walks are not going to come back. Dump cycles

Sunset from the DJAC terrace is unmissable

PoRs give you a family too (if you aren’t de-ratified)

Academics is a garden everyone has to plough.

Elections are ugly.

L18-19 ground with mild sunshine is Peak in winters

Giving 100% for your GC pool is not bad at all

You’re not uncool if you don’t smoke/drink

Avoid proxy attendance. Befriend Biometric wale bhaiya

Never miss any pronite.

Taking up one sport/Gym/hobby is real fun

For Movies: Rave Moti >> Z square

Crunchy Frappe will never be available in CCD. It’s been 2 years.

Prof. Nitin Kaistha is the greatest boon for the student community

Final Chapter - The Story of My Life

The story of my life, I take her home
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
I drive all night to keep her warm.

The final ride was bittersweet. This place was more than an institution now.

Countless moments when I stumbled, failed, and was losing hope. But this place and its air silently whispered that you cannot relax, the Show must go on. It never promised an easy path, never promised success but it always promised – second chances.

It challenged me, developed me and, for the good, humbled me. We never realize this ecosystem never asks for anything but gracefully gives – memories, friends, experiences, team spirit, lore, opportunities and most importantly – betterment.

I doubt if one can love a place as deeply as one loves a person. But,

I realized it was “her”. It was IIT Kanpur.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #79 : Uncanny Paths to One Direction]
It is our destiny to give her the warmth, and respect she deserves.
No matter how good or bad you are, she will still love you for the ages. She will bless you immensely and never ask back.

The story of my life is not merely that I studied here.

It is that I belonged here. I am blessed.

But you, my champion. Can you do a thing for me ?

For all that you can, Give back to the Campus!

Until then,

Goodbye, folks !

Written by: Yash Giri
Edited by: Moksh Dalal, Anandan Iyer

=== As We Leave #80: Portrait of a Time ===
Source: https://voxiitk.com/as-we-leave-80-portrait-of-a-time/
Date: 2026-08-01

In this edition of As We Leave, Pranav Agrawal, a Y22 student from the Department of Physics, reflects on four transformative years at IIT Kanpur, sharing how embracing uncertainty, finding a home in Vox, making lifelong friendships, and saying yes to unexpected opportunities shaped his journey. Through stories of personal growth, impromptu plans and the people who stood by him, he celebrates the conviction to take leaps of faith, discover oneself, and cherish the memories that made IITK feel like home.

Disclaimer: The views presented below are the author’s own and are not in any manner representative of the views of Vox Populi as a body or IIT Kanpur in general. This is an informal account of the author’s experiences at IITK.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #80: Portrait of a Time]
As I sat down to write this essay, I realised I was writing it for the version of me that arrived on campus four years ago. I keep past versions of myself in my memory. The fact that I’ve become someone my 18-year-old self would have been proud of means everything to me. A lot has changed over time, but there’s still a few things that have remained constant. As an ode to artists that have stayed with me through multiple chapters of my life, and to loosely delineate this essay, I will shamelessly borrow one of PCRC’s songs and its lyrics to organise this As We Leave :P.

🎶 … I wasn’t breakin’ any rules
What has IITK taught me? I think it’s conviction.

You come in after what probably felt like inevitable decisions one after another, and suddenly, you’re thrown into a world where you’re accountable for every choice you made? The horrors. The world in all its glory unfolds beyond you, and you’re grasping for any handhold at all. Of course, you look for people to emulate, but what do they know? They’re as scared as you with their newfound freedom. Besides, they’re not you, you with your strengths and idiosyncrasies.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #80: Portrait of a Time]
I remember being worried about a lot. Which is valid, because damn did those decisions look huge looming in the horizon. But I made it through each one. Each decision, each phase, I made it out with just a little more self-belief than before. Maybe the worst-case that I was catastrophizing about did happen. So what? I’m alive to tell the tale aren’t I? Fu*k, I broke my leg and I was fine anyway. Que sera, sera.

So I say, be brave and take those leaps of faith. It took a long time for me to come to terms with this. I saw people fake it till they made it. There is something visceral that changes within you when you see someone go the distance. You can do it too! You would be surprised at what you can achieve. Do the crazy thing you were slightly hesitant about. Fight God while you’re at it.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #80: Portrait of a Time]
Here I am on the other side. I wouldn’t have dreamed of the opportunities that came my way, and I am glad that past me had the guts to take initiative and say yes every time.

🎶 … I was someone so blind
I had planned a lot subconsciously before coming to college. I wanted to hit the ground running. The clubs I wanted to join, the sports I wanted to play, bucket list items that I set about checking off as soon as I reached campus. But IITK has a way of throwing curveballs your way that you could never account for (PE111 fakka gang rise up). I remember that I joined Vox not because of any sort of premeditation, but because they rolled out their forms first, and it just sounded so exciting. I was sure that this is where I’d wanted to be from the very first interview itself. It was my found family with the coolest seniors I’ve met.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #80: Portrait of a Time]
I’ve had the opportunity throughout my tenure to dig deep into every stakeholder across campus, whether it’s the staff that made IITK function, the professors, or the student body. It gave me the chance to appreciate our college for what it is. Vox sits at a unique position with a critical eye to all that is happening across campus, and it helped me on many occasions to step back and evaluate my own notions. Compromise is complicated, and nuance is everywhere.

Coming back to theme, I was soon lost. All these new choices and I do believe I lost sight of my dreams for a bit as I blindly figured out how to orient myself in the whirlwind that was all my conflicting commitments through second and third year. Add to this rejections and circumstance, and suddenly there were things out of my reach – door closed. Cue lots of soul searching. You see, everyone says chase your dreams, as if they’re these clearly defined milestones shining in the distance and all you gotta do is run. But the truth is, it takes a lot of groundwork to gain that clarity. It is okay to take out time for this. Find yourself. Find your direction.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #80: Portrait of a Time]
There were times I was absolutely paralysed with confusion, rocked by the intensity of emotions I felt. Somewhere near the end, I found a philosophy that I swear by now. I spent a lot of early college waiting for certainty, but I think you must decide with whatever you got at the moment and let the certainty arrive later. The wins will come.

The stars, and all the gravy… 🎶
Locations hold memories. There will always be a soft spot in my heart for Gupta’s. That man served me the best sliced mangoes I’ve eaten in my life, every summer, including the summer in we were forced to stay on campus in throughout. My Bangalore ass got body slammed by this city’s 47 degrees Celsius in 2023. The only thing I remember from that summer was having Gupta ji’s mangoes. For Hall 4’s canteen and the comfort dinners there. For B-Mid in Hall 2, with the trees across the wing. The anarchy that was Hall 1. These places hold imprints of my daily rituals, time spent well. Long after the people are gone, the places will hold those memories.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #80: Portrait of a Time]
I used to wonder why people romanticise their college experience, as I am. The answer probably comes down to spontaneity. It’s not isolated to campus itself: there is magic in unplanned moments. I travelled loads throughout college, nearly all of it yolo. One moment in particular sticks out to me though. We were in Thailand, and we’d decided to go to Koh Phangan, which was this sleepy hippie island and when I tell you those two days had me re-evaluating my entire life? Sometimes you see glimmers of another lifestyle and wonder what it could’ve been. While IITK can feel vast, it is at the end of the day a small town of 10,000 people from the same genre. It’s easy to assume that everyone follows the same trajectory – but no, running a tiny community gaming café third space also sounds like a very appealing way of life to me now. Hey, at least I learned how to travel well, another lifestyle or no. The trick is to be very type B and offbeat about it.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #80: Portrait of a Time]
Looking back, because home was so far away, there were multiple instances where I didn’t visit for almost a year. IITK filled that space for me. The campus is alive and buzzing throughout the day (and night). You can always walk into your friend’s room right next door or go to OAT at 3 A.M. to find someone you know. Countless random tiny interactions make for fond memories. I will take this opportunity to thank those closest to me on campus – the last semester was an absolute roller coaster, and I know I only thrived despite the broken leg because of the invaluable support my wing mates came together to give me. Looking back, I can only see silver linings, and that’s nothing short of a miracle conjured up by my friends. D-Bot, you have my heart <3.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #80: Portrait of a Time]
As I graduate, I feel so incredibly lucky to meet the people I did during my four years. I came in with such a narrow perspective of the world, a simplistic idea of everything. Luckily for me, IITK is a melting pot of perspectives, and to the very last day, I met people that come from incredibly different backgrounds who do things so so differently. It was eye opening, especially since the Bangalore I know is relatively homogenous in comparison. I’ve had the privilege to be friends with some incredibly passionate people, all pursuing radically different paths. Thank you for showing me that ambition can take many forms.

I was fortunate to be surrounded by exceptional mentors. I’m also very privileged to receive the kind of agency I did. It was weird travelling 36 hours across the world to get home, but I got like, the budget international student experience (cultural whiplash included :)). I felt like the main character haha.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #80: Portrait of a Time]
And finally, a thank you to my 18-year-old self for making it here. Strap in baby, you are not going to believe the crazy shit that goes down. You’re way stronger than you give yourself credit for. It is going to be worth it. Though if any of you readers are from PHY, my thoughts and prayers are with you.

Oh I’m runnin’ in, somewhere else to you, oh… 🎶
I consider myself a pragmatic person. I’m not someone who believes in magic for example. Nor do I believe in a benevolent god overlooking my life and deciding to give it a friendly push.

And yet. It was a movie. It was beyond anything I could’ve imagined. For a moment, we really had it all. It’s going to be hard for me to match the sheer novelty and breadth of experiences I had.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #80: Portrait of a Time]
There’s a quartet for the four years I experienced at IITK; I’d like to close with those lines:

__________________________________________________________________________________

We shall not cease from exploration
And the end of all our exploring
Will be to arrive where we started
And know the place for the first time.

~ TS Eliot

It was an honour, Kanpur 🙂

Signing off,
Pranav Agrawal – 220791

=== As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward ===
Source: https://voxiitk.com/as-we-leave-81-connecting-the-dots-backward/
Date: 2026-08-06

In the 81st edition of As We Leave 2026, Abhishek, a Y22 from the Department of Computer Science and Engineering, reflects on four transformative years of turning setbacks into stepping stones at IIT Kanpur. From overcoming an initial academic shock to navigating the highs and lows of the placement season, he shares how stepping out of his comfort zone, relying on a tight-knit network of friends, and embracing life’s unexpected curveballs helped him evolve from a quiet JEE aspirant into a resilient, confident graduate.

Writing this AWL feels incredibly nostalgic. For the past few years, around this time, I would sit and read my seniors’ AWLs, thinking I would write mine someday – and that “someday” has finally arrived.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
During the grueling days of JEE prep, IIT Kanpur was just a montage of YouTube campus tours and fest aftermovies – a distant dream I watched on a screen just to keep my motivation alive. But crossing that screen and actually stepping into this world was a completely different reality. If you had told the wide-eyed, introverted Abhishek who walked through these gates four years ago how his story would end, he wouldn’t have believed you. I arrived carrying a lot of dreams, but I was also someone who found it incredibly hard to simply say “no” to people. Over the years, I actively sought out advice to improve different aspects of my personality, and actually applying it worked wonders for me. I quickly realized that while our institute is revered for its academics, the absolute greatest library you will ever have access to is the people and the situations around you. Every random conversation, every late-night debate, and every hurdle was a lesson. My journey didn’t start with instant perfection – in fact, stumbling early on was the exact push I needed to shatter my comfort zone. Before we walk through the timeline of these four years, just know this: the transformation from an isolated JEE aspirant to the person leaving today wasn’t always easy, but it was profoundly worth it.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
First Semester: The Awakening

I would like to take it back to my JEE days, when I used to watch campus tours and fest aftermovies just to fuel my motivation to reach an IIT. Fortunately, the hard work paid off, and I secured my admission into CSE at IIT Kanpur. Looking back, I had no idea that the curious, introverted guy who stepped onto campus on October 27, 2022, would undergo such a massive transformation by the time he left this beautiful place.
I entered college with a quiet determination to break out of my shell, and orientation week was the perfect catalyst. It was a stress-free time with pure fun, meeting new people, and the hilarious task of “covering gaps in the line” 😆. My fondest memories from those early days are a mix of chaos and connection: singing in the auditorium, shooting a Harlem Shake video, and late-night jamming sessions in the wing.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
Then, academics hit. The very first PHY114 class was a wake-up call, a clear signal that this wasn’t going to be easy. The first few weeks were an intense academic shock, including a flat zero score on my very first quiz. But instead of letting it bring me down, I somehow found myself enjoying the struggle. I was embracing the hustle of this new phase.
I made sure to balance the academic grind with everything campus life had to offer. Sports and late-night games became an inseparable part of hostel life. I played some games like squash for the first time in my life. Dragging myself out of bed into the freezing campus fog for morning PE was a struggle, but those early games ended up being incredibly fun. I thoroughly enjoyed Fresher’s Night, pulled my first all-nighter in the treasure hunt, celebrated birthdays and New Year’s, campaigned for Pallav, and connected with a bunch of people. Because I was genuinely enjoying the journey, I felt a deep sense of satisfaction when the semester wrapped up. I was happy both academically and non-academically, but more importantly, I could feel a tangible, positive shift in my personality.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
More than anything, the first semester gave me my people. I was incredibly lucky to find a group of friends who quickly became like family and stuck by my side for all four years. We crashed a wedding at the Community Centre and scaled almost every campus rooftop. I explored all the classic study spots – the Library, SL, NCL, and RM. We also pulled a few all-nighters, not to study, but purely for fun, strolling through the quiet campus and making memories. Looking back at those times, I realized our seniors were absolutely right: the first semester truly is one of the greatest times of your college journey.

Second Sem : The Juggling Act

The second semester brought a completely different energy. I attended my first college fest, and it felt surreal-I was now actually a part of the very fest aftermovies I used to watch for motivation during my JEE days. I dove headfirst into campus activities, taking part in Dramatics for Galaxy and a Consulting PS for Takneek. Between these commitments and the endless stream of recruitment tasks for various clubs, teams, and SnT projects, my sleep schedule went completely out the window. For the first time, I felt genuinely exhausted.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
This phase gave me my first real taste of the campus interview grind, as I sat for SnT clubs, teams, SG, and AM selections. I managed to get into Humanoid and ICG, and ended up taking on three or four different projects across various entities. Suddenly, a massive chunk of my time was swallowed up by project tasks and meetings.
The biggest challenge of the semester wasn’t any single assignment, but rather learning the art of balancing everything-projects, academics, and, crucially, time with friends. I quickly realized that taking on too much was unsustainable, which led to the tough decision to leave Humanoid after just two months to ease my load. Looking back, I deeply cherish that choice because it gave me the breathing room I desperately needed.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
Despite the packed schedule, the fun never stopped. Escaping the campus heat for a trip to the waterpark and going on long, aimless walks across campus with my friends remain some of my fondest memories from this period.
Ultimately, the second semester taught me how to juggle multiple responsibilities simultaneously without losing myself in the chaos. And just like that, with a mix of exhaustion and triumph, we said goodbye to our first year.

Third Semester: A New Beginning

Entering the third semester, there was a sudden shift in the air-a looming sense of seriousness. Listening to our seniors’ internship stories was a real wake-up call that it was time to buckle down. Seeing my friends start the Competitive Programming grind, I decided to jump in and start coding alongside them.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
This was also the semester when our core department courses began, which naturally shifted my social dynamic. My interactions with non-department friends were reduced, remaining mostly limited to my wingies, the clubs I was part of, and the people I regularly played sports with. Meanwhile, my bond with the department crowd and my wingies grew stronger, and I ended up making some great new friends along the way.
Academically, things leveled up. We got our first real taste of assignments, and surprisingly, I actually found solving them really enjoyable.
Despite the growing pressure, the semester was packed with unforgettable moments. Cycling to Ganga Barrage, soaking in the vibe of my second Antaragni, the unique experience of a take-home endsem exam, and the legendary CS201 course dinners hosted by Manindra Sir all hold a special place in my heart. Celebrating Diwali on campus, conducting AM class, and just having fun with friends balanced out the workload. In many ways, the third semester felt like a completely new start.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
Fourth Semester: The Halfway Mark

The fourth semester was bookended by two memorable trips to Lucknow-one right at the beginning to kick things off, and another just after the semester ended to celebrate. This was a semester of pushing my boundaries and trying new things. I ran the first 5K run of my life, participated in the Institute Phatta League, and even took lawn tennis lessons (though that only lasted for about a month!). It was a time filled with sports, laughter, and simply enjoying life with my friends.
On the academic side, the CS253 Software project took center stage. The late nights and the overall journey of building that project together with my friends remains one of my absolute fondest college memories.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
When this semester finally came to a close, marking the official halfway point of my college life, it truly hit me. Taking a moment to reflect, I could actually feel how much I had grown up since day one. By the end of second year, campus no longer felt unfamiliar-it felt like home. I was stepping into the second half of my college journey significantly more confident, and stronger both mentally and emotionally.

Third Year

The summer leading into my third year was dominated by internship prep.Staying in constant touch with my friends and seniors kept me motivated to push through the grind. I also co-mentored two projects during this time. Facing a few initial rejections was tough but  all the hard work paid off when, right at the start of the fifth semester, I landed an internship at Glean.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
The days immediately following that offer were some of the best of my college life-just pure relief and chilling with friends. But the chilling phase didn’t last long. The fifth semester is notorious as a core CSE heavy-hitter, and I had overloaded my schedule. Naturally, a massive chunk of my time was swallowed by academics.
Yet, looking back, the academic grind is exactly what brought us closer. My favorite memories are studying together in KD Lab, taking much-needed evening tea breaks at the CC canteen, heading out of campus for dinners, and playing games with my wingies while laughing at completely senseless jokes. To top it all off, this semester gave me one of my favorite college trips ever: an unforgettable getaway to Manali, filled with stunning views, bonfires, adventure and mafia games with friends.”
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
Then came the sixth semester. With the pressure of securing an internship off my shoulders, I let loose and started playing even more.I played badminton and cricket almost regularly, engaged in intense Mafia games, and participated in the Hall 12 Phatta league and a futsal league with my wingies. We also religiously maintained our daily ritual of shaam ki chai throughout the 8th sem . A major highlight was taking a trip to Prayagraj to experience the Mahakumbh.
Academically, this semester was project-heavy. In fact, this was the very first semester where I actually had to pull a genuine all-nighter for academics! Exam preparation usually happened just a few days before the papers, but the projects consumed most of my time. Before I knew it, the semester wrapped up, and it was time to step into the corporate world.

[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
The Summer Internship

My internship was an incredible time-my very first real corporate experience. Stepping into that world gave me a profound sense of independence. I loved the fast-paced environment and working alongside incredibly smart people, getting my hands dirty with real code .
The demanding work was perfectly balanced with an amazing culture. I interacted with students and professionals from all over the world, traveled and hiked extensively, and made wonderful new friends. I was lucky to have excellent housemates who taught me so much, and I even played golf for the first time in my life!
It felt like a dream summer. But coming back to campus posed a completely new, unexpected challenge. I received the news that I didn’t get the Pre-Placement Offer (PPO).
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
It was a curveball I wasn’t prepared for at all…

Seventh Semester

The news of not getting the PPO was a heavy blow. With it, my long-held dream of going on a semester exchange program to EPFL shattered completely. Suddenly, I found myself back at square one, gearing up for the intense, high-pressure placement season.
But this is where the magic of the people around me truly shined. My friends and my entire environment were incredibly supportive. Thanks to them, the stress faded just a few days after the news broke. Instead of panicking, I surprisingly found myself enjoying the process all over again.
I tried to carve out an hour every evening to play cricket and clear my head. My friend Ansh’s room essentially became the official common room for our wing – we spent hours in there relieving stress through hilarious, completely random conversations and endless card games.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
Then came the turnaround. Fortunately, I secured a placement at an amazing company for a role that aligns much more with my interests. It is a path I genuinely enjoy far more than what I was doing during my internship.
It is often said that you can’t connect the dots looking forward; you can only connect them looking backward. Standing here now, I feel that with absolute certainty. Not getting that PPO was a bitter pill to swallow at the time, but it ended up being one of the absolute best things that could have ever happened to me.

Eighth Semester

If the seventh semester was about connecting the dots, the eighth semester was simply about celebrating the picture they created. It was, hands down, the best semester of my entire college life. With the heavy pressures finally behind us, we went all out. We traveled to the ghats of Varanasi, and went on an unforgettable trek to Kedarkantha-that pre-dawn summit push is a memory that will be etched in my mind forever.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
Campus life turned into an endless string of games and activities. I played a lot of cricket, badminton, and poker. My wingies and I teamed up for late night volleyball and played our own across-hall treasure hunt. I also decided it was time to try something completely new and picked up the guitar. I also tried wall climbing for the first time and ran my 2nd 5K run.
On the academic side, I found a deeply rewarding routine in tutoring the ESC course throughout both my 7th and 8th semesters, giving back to the juniors. But the nights were strictly reserved for making memories. We danced our hearts out at Dance Extravaganza and our department farewell, took over the mics at karaoke night, and partied harder than we ever had before.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
As the final days ticked down, the air grew heavy with nostalgia. I spent my time reconnecting with old friends, capturing countless memories through photographs, and covering shirts in heartfelt messages on Scribble Day. It was a semester of pure, unadulterated joy with my people. And honestly, having that much fun together is exactly what made the final goodbye so incredibly hard.

CONCLUSION

Looking back at this incredible journey, I am overwhelmed with gratitude for this vibrant campus-a place that is magically just as alive and buzzing in the middle of the night as it is during the day. I am profoundly thankful to the professors who guided me, and to the friends who sat with me through truly life-changing discussions. IITK didn’t just give me a degree; it gave me a new family. I found friends who were always, unconditionally there for me.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
When I think about the true essence of this journey, it is found in those shared everyday moments: walking to the canteen together every single day, laughing endlessly at completely random things, and surviving the chaos of group projects. From getting consoled by them during my lowest heartbreaks to celebrating our biggest successes side-by-side, and all the unforgettable trips and game nights in between-they made every single second of these four years truly memorable.
If I can leave my juniors with a few pieces of advice from my “library” of experiences, it would be this: First, never be afraid of failing. Embrace your setbacks. As Sundar Pichai said, wear your failure as a badge of honor. As Roger Federer’s advice says: even at the absolute peak of his career, he only won 54% of the points he played. His lesson was simple—you are going to lose points, and you must learn to quickly let them go.  Whether it was taking a flat zero on my very first quiz or the sudden shock of not getting a PPO, those perceived “failures” were actually the defining moments that pushed me toward much greater successes. Always remember that the dots only connect looking backward.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
Second, dive in and ruthlessly explore the boundless opportunities this campus provides. Sometimes the perfect opportunity will land right in your lap, but other times, you have to be brave enough to go out and create it yourself.
Yet, amidst all that hustle, protect your peace and energy. I am an ardent believer in the Pomodoro technique-not just for studying, but as a life philosophy. Value your breaks, step away when you need to, and enjoy the journey. Whenever I felt completely overwhelmed by a mountain of projects and deadlines, using this technique was my anchor.
Third, help others unconditionally; the sheer positivity it brings back into your life is unmatched. Finally, learn the quiet power of saying “no,” and always, always preserve your individuality, no matter whose company you find yourself in.
[Context: VOX POPULI (VOX), STUDENT MEDIA BODY OF IIT KANPUR - ARTICLES PUBLISHED IN 2026 > As We Leave #81: Connecting the Dots Backward]
Ultimately, no journey is ever a perfect, straight line. It is a beautiful, chaotic mix of soaring highs, crushing lows, unexpected heartbreaks, and hard-earned successes. But every single one of those moments was necessary. The curious Abhishek who entered these gates four years ago would be proud of the person leaving today. I am walking away transformed. Thank you, IIT Kanpur, for the holistic growth, the endless opportunities, and the absolute best memories of my life.

Written by: Abhishek Khandelwal
